5b:["$","p","p-29",{"children":"For proteins, our body’s efficiency is about ~23%. That means for every 1g of protein, which has 4kcal, we need around ~17kcal to make it. Think of it like this: 4kcal goes into the actual amino acids in the protein, and the other 13kcal is the energy used up in the building process."}]
5c:["$","p","p-30",{"children":"Now, lipids are a different story. Our body’s a bit more efficient here, at ~93%. So for 1g of lipids, which has 9kcal, it only takes about 10kcal to make it. That’s 9kcal for the amino acids in the lipids, and just 1kcal for the synthesis."}]
5d:["$","h3","h3-6",{"id":"calculating-the-energy-cost","children":["Calculating the energy cost",["$","a","a-0",{"className":"heading-anchor","aria-label":"Link","href":"#calculating-the-energy-cost","children":"#"}]]}]

66:["$","h2","h2-4",{"id":"calculating-the-tdee","children":["Calculating the TDEE",["$","a","a-0",{"className":"heading-anchor","aria-label":"Link","href":"#calculating-the-tdee","children":"#"}]]}]

68:["$","p","p-37",{"children":"To calculate your TDEE, follow these steps:"}]
69:["$","ol","ol-0",{"children":["\n",["$","li","li-0",{"children":"Get the total number of calories consumed during the 30-day period."}],"\n",["$","li","li-1",{"children":"Calculate the weight difference between the start and end of the 30-day period."}],"\n",["$","li","li-2",{"children":"Convert the weight difference to calories by multiplying it by the number of muscle/fat gained/lost."}],"\n",["$","li","li-3",{"children":"Subtract the result from step 3 from the total calories consumed in the 30-day period."}],"\n",["$","li","li-4",{"children":"Divide the result from step 4 by 30 to get your average daily calorie intake, which should be your TDEE."}],"\n"]}]
